# PREFACE

*This third and final volume of the Magical Papyrus contains a Demotic Glossary and various indices. Originally we had the intention of adding a chapter on the grammar of the text, as is indicated by a few references made to it in the notes of Vol. I., but various circumstances have prevented its fulfilment. We much regret the long delay in the appearance of the present volume.*

F. Ll. G.
H. T.

<vocab>Magical Papyrus, Demotic Glossary, indices, grammar, F. Ll. G., H. T.</vocab>

# Alphabet for the Glossary..

| | | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. | / | a | 11. | / | l |
| * 2. | { // | e | 12. | ρ, g | ḥ, ḫ |
| | { 2), \|, <\ | ’, ’, c | 13. | 6, b | ẖ |
| 3. | /// | y | 14. | q, y, <\\ | s |
| 4. | 3, ρ | w | 15. | λ, 3 | š |
| 5. | 4, lh | b | 16. | σ, w, v | k, g |
| 6. | µ, 2 | p | 17. | < | q |
| 7. | y | f | 18. | <, S | t |
| 8. | 3 | m | 19. | // | θ |
| 9. | d, — | n | 20. | \|+ | z |
| 10. | / | r | | | |

\* As initial letters these two sub-groups have been kept distinct . Elsewhere no distinction has been maintained in the order of their arrangement .

Note.—Each word has been numbered for convenience of reference.

Feminine words with final <**t** are entered without regard to the <**t** .

Compounds are entered immediately after the first component of the word.

All forms of the definite article will be found under µ **p**, of the demonstrative under µ **py**, and so forth.

All figures following a **V.** in the same line refer to the **Verso**.

Coptic words are quoted in the order Sah. : Boh.

"pap. Ryl." refers to a forthcoming publication of the Catalogue of the John Rylands Demotic Papyri by F.Ll. Griffith.

<vocab>Alphabet, Glossary, Demotic, Coptic, Sahidic, Bohairic, John Rylands Demotic Papyri, F.Ll. Griffith, transliteration</vocab>

> The form <image-desc>cursive sign</image-desc> (hier. forms <image-desc>Eye of Horus/ir-glyph</image-desc> 27/10, <image-desc>hieroglyphs</image-desc> (?) 13/2) is used for the <term>stm-f</term> and relative forms, for the infinitive, absolute and construct, and usually for the infinitive with pronominal suffix (5/14, 24/11 etc.), but occasionally the last-named form seems to be written as <term>iy-f</term> (<image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> 21/35, V. 12/5), <term>iy.t-f</term> (<image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> V. 12/5) and even <term>iy-y.t-f</term> (<image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> V. 12/6); see no. 28 above and cf. copt. <term>αα : αιο</term>. The imperative form is <image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> <term>a.iry</term> copt. <term>αρι</term> (2/26, 7/7, 8/6, V. 14/6); but the infinitive is also used as an imperative (10/32, 24/28, V. 3/3, 18/10)

The meanings are very various and may be roughly classified as follows: —

I. used absolutely a/ 19/24(?). 25(3) (meaning doubtful)
->b/ **ir n** "amount to" 12/4.8, 24/24<-

II. followed by direct object: —
> a/ "make" e.g. light 2/17; blood (i.e. become bloody) 5/28, 27/28; magic 23/7, V. 12/2; justice 2/26; remedy 20/24, healing 20/23, ointment 29/5; bandage 6/13; invocation 16/27, 29/1; inquiry 27/13, 28/10; to offer a prayer 10/24, 27/2; send a message 5/29, 27/24. 29; give an answer 7/7. 23, 10/8, 28/8, a command V. 26/4, a revelation 5/14; bring a dream V. 17/4; <term>urinam reddere</term> V. 5/4
> 
> b/ "pass (time)" 3/1, 12/13

241. <image-desc>Demotic character for b</image-desc> **b** negative particle of the praesens consuet.

> (1) followed by the **stm-f** form of **rh** (sometimes written **ir-rh**)
> Sah. **МЕШЕ=** 9/4, 21/39.41
>
> (2) followed by the **stm-f** form of **ir** (auxiliary) Sah. **МЕРЕ=**
> 14/18, 17/10.26.28, 22/3 (cf. 8/5 invocation word)
>
> more usually preceded by **n e** 5/12.17, 7/9.12, 17/2.5.27.30, 19/7
> in one case 17/2 it is written phonetically <image-desc>group of Demotic characters</image-desc> **emy-s** Sah. **ЕМЕС**
>
> see also no. 242

<meta>Underlined "m" in the top-left margin acts as an alphabetical guide. The page contains a handwritten lexicon of Demotic Egyptian terms starting with "m", including transliterations, meanings, citations, and parallels in Hieroglyphic, Coptic, and Greek.</meta>

## 3 = m

338. <image-desc>Demotic character resembling a large handwritten numeral 3.</image-desc> **m** prep. "in, from, as" **copt. N** passim
    <image-desc>Vertical stroke and hook.</image-desc> **hier. form** 11/11-14. 17, 23/30, V.22/6
    <image-desc>Sign sequence resembling "p3 3".</image-desc> **mw** Apparently this may represent simple "**m**" from " 11/3, 11/15 (*mw-hn* "in")
    13/3 ("because"(?)). Perhaps it = **m wε** in 1/2 (cf. **mw = na** in **ΠNAC TOP** 2/17).
    It varies with **m'** (<image-desc>Sign resembling an L crossed by a diagonal stroke.</image-desc> below) in **ryz mw ryz** 2/16, **lot mw lot** 18/13,
    **lil mw lil** V.12/3 .
    **mw-ky** seems to be a compound for "like" 3/17, 5/14
    <image-desc>Sign resembling an L with a crossbar and a dot above.</image-desc> **hier. form** (<image-desc>Drawing of an owl hieroglyph.</image-desc>) **m'** "like" 10/27, 27/5

339. <image-desc>Sign resembling a '3' followed by two circles with a bar over them.</image-desc> **m'** verb "see" 5/20, 7/15.16, V.22/12.16.[20]
    <image-desc>Variation of the 'see' verb sign.</image-desc> 5/19, 7/15, 11/2, 29/19

340. <image-desc>Curved Demotic sign followed by the digits 203.</image-desc> **m'** subst. "semen(?)" 11/8

341. <image-desc>Demotic sign sequence roughly resembling '< 313 203'.</image-desc> **m'.t** subst. f. "urine(?)" in phrase **'r ty-s m'.t** Sah. **PMH** "pass water"
    21/22, V.5/5, but perhaps in the former instance it may = "weep" S. **TPMHI**
    *cf. no 415*

342. <image-desc>Sign resembling a hook '7' followed by '3'.</image-desc> **ma** subst. m. "place" **copt. Ma** 3/28.30, 4/4, 5/25.26.29, 6/7, 10/10.34,
    11/25, 12/12.31, 13/7.16.21, 14/31, 15/17.18.20, 16/23.28, 17/16, 21/27.32.39.41, 23/22.31/
    25/16.18, 27/19.25.26.29, 28/4, 29/21, V.9/9, 12/9, 13/3, 16/4, 17/7, 19/6, 29/6, 32/3.4

343. <image-desc>Sign resembling a mirrored L and a '3'.</image-desc> **mε.t** subst. f. "truth" ME: **MHI** 1/27, 2/27, 9/22
    <image-desc>Truth sign with a horizontal bar beneath.</image-desc> **n mε.t** used as an adj. "true" Sah. **MME** 1/27, 5/5, 6/2.8, 9/14.16.29,
    10/11.32, 11/7.9.16, 12/2, 14/15.18, 16/23, 17/22, 20/19.21.22, 21/20.33, 23/29, 25/12,

# The Cipher

As W. Groff first demonstrated in his memoir on this papyrus called "Etudes sur la Sorcellerie" (Mém. de l'inst. égypt. III p. 337 seq. esp. pp. 358. 370), the key to the cipher is contained in some parallel passages, where words, evidently equivalent, are written in the one case in cipher and in the other in demotic.

These passages are :—

$\alpha)$ 10/31 = 27/9 where <image-desc>group of several cipher characters including shapes like 4, 3, λ, and vertical strokes</image-desc> = <image-desc>group of demotic characters</image-desc> copt. <term>ⲥⲙⲟⲩⲛⲉ</term>
and <image-desc>group of cipher characters including mirrored shapes and vertical bars</image-desc> = <image-desc>group of demotic characters</image-desc> copt. <term>ⲕⲟⲩⲕⲟⲩⲫⲁⲧ</term>
and F . . . . = <image-desc>group of demotic characters</image-desc> (copt. <term>ⲙⲟⲩⲗⲁϩ</term>)
$\beta)$ 5/25 = 27/25 where <image-desc>three cipher signs including a hook and 'TY'</image-desc> = <image-desc>three demotic signs</image-desc> copt. <term>ⲃⲁⲗ</term>
$\gamma)$ 24/26 and 38 where <image-desc>four cipher signs resembling vertical bars and a hook</image-desc> = <image-desc>three demotic signs</image-desc> copt. <term>ⲛ̄ⲥⲁⲧ</term>

These equivalents furnish the values of 13 signs, which, when applied to the groups in 24/7. 10 shew that these clearly represent the two substances written out in demotic just below in ll. 18. 19 viz. <term>μανδραγόρα</term> and <term>κισσός</term> ; and so values are obtained for four more signs. Confirmation is afforded by the group in 24/9 resolving itself at once into the greek word <term>ὑοσκυάμου</term> etc. As a result we obtain the following list of signs with their values expressed in Coptic letters . The numbers refer to the list of words in cipher on page 109 seq.

| Sign | Coptic equivalent | Remarks |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| i — | ⲁ | = dem. <image-desc>small horizontal hook or wedge-shaped demotic sign</image-desc> cf. 63 and demot. glossary no. 725 |
| ii F | ⲁ | value from 61 (= gk. ⲁ) |
| iii F,F | ⲉ | The numerous instances in which this sign ends a word, as well as its constant interchange with no. iv shows that it is ⲉ rather than ⲁ, the value suggested by 28 |
| iv IK | ⲉ | Value from 62 ; interchanges with iii and v |
| v T | ⲉ, ⲡ(?) | the value ⲉ from 6 = dem. <image-desc>demotic sign consisting of two vertical strokes</image-desc> in dem. gloss. no. 247<br>the value ⲡ(?) from 29. 30 |

| xxv | it | π | value from 28 |

| xxxv | b (?) | ⲃ | occurs only in 60; cf. glosses 9/6, 29/10 |
| xxxvi | <image-desc>v-like shape with a horizontal bar</image-desc> | ϭ, ⲕ | { = dem. ϭ ⲕ in dem. gloss. no. 916<br>{ = gk. ⲕ in 17.32 and ɣ in 83 |

As appears from the above table there may be more than one sign for a given value, so that one and the same word may be written in different ways, cf. nos 88 and 89, 46 and 47 etc. Furthermore one sign may have more than one value. Signs ii and xxx are undistinguishable in form, but this is the only instance of two wholly incompatible values, unless the value π, occurring in two instances of one word, be accepted as free from error for no. v; moreover in one or two instances no xiv approximates in form to no. v.

The alternative values given under xiii and xiv are closely related, since ɣ in the glosses frequently represents both demotic e (7/22. 25. 28, 16/3 etc.) and the aspirate (evidently owing to the regular aspiration of initial υ in Greek) e.g.

->ɣ = dem. <image-desc>curved hook symbol</image-desc> h in 1/15.25, 2/16, 5/11.20, 7/25 etc.<-
->= dem. <image-desc>loop symbol</image-desc> h in 7/33, 8/2.3.5.6<-
->= dem. <image-desc>hook symbol</image-desc> h in 1/10.9/13<-

<vocab>demotic, Coptic, Greek, glosses, aspiration, transcription, paleography, Leiden Papyrus</vocab>

--- Page 116 ---
<language>English</language>
<page-num>108</page-num>
<page-type>text</page-type>

The two values T and <term>$\chi$</term> for xxix (cf. also nos. 20. 21) are likewise akin, since both represent in coptic words written in the old language with <term>$\text{demotic sign}$</term> and <term>$\text{demotic sign}$</term>; and greek transcripts reproduce <term>$\chi$</term> by T, <term>$\theta$</term>.

A fragment of what looks like the same cipher (//// RTF) is found in Pap. 3229 of the Louvre (Maspero, *Quelques papyrus*, pl. II. 25), but it is incomplete owing to a hole in the page and is insufficient to form a known word.

Four of the signs viz. <term>$\tau$</term>, <term>$\equiv$</term>, <term>III</term>, <term>n</term> appear in another secret alphabet written on the verso of col. 25 of our papyrus (see Leemans facsimile, plate XIV), apparently with the values A, Z, N, W, if the signs be taken to correspond to the order and values of the greek alphabet, and these are the same values as they have here.

Of the remaining signs five are merely demotic letters, but slightly modified, viz. <term>y</term>, <term>6</term>, <term>6</term>, <term>L</term>, <term>3</term> ; <term>$\text{h-sign}$</term> is found on a mummy ticket (Spiegelberg, *Eigennamen* p. 18-19) with the values <term>$\eta$</term> and <term>$\alpha$</term>, derived according to Spiegelberg from <term>$\text{demotic sign}$</term>. In our papyrus it has the value of 5. <term>y</term> and <term>y'</term> (B) are probably the same and are merely variations of <term>y</term>(F); cf. 3BE as gloss to <term>$\text{demotic sign for "life"}$</term> life 29/6. Some letters seem to be greek letters turned upside down, e.g. .x. xii. xiii. xix.

The cipher was probably greek in origin, that is, was invented by a Greek, because (1) a certain number of the signs are demotic and represent those sounds in the egyptian language which are wanting in the Greek language. Thus they would afford some concealment in greek writing, but none in demotic; (2) there are some signs <term>$\zeta$</term>, <term>$\Sigma$</term>, <term>$\equiv$</term> which occur only in greek words and which therefore may have conveyed to greek ears a different sound from any letters in egyptian; (3) there is no special sign for the aspirate, vowel signs being used instead; (4) there is no differentiation between the sounds T and <term>$\chi$</term>, which an Egyptian could scarcely have failed to distinguish

The above conclusion is in harmony also with the inference, resting on other grounds (vol. I, p. 11-12) that the magical papyrus is an egyptian version of a greek original, though no doubt this earlier greek version was itself indebted for most of its material at any rate to egyptian sources.

<vocab>coptic, demotic, greek, egyptian, cipher, papyrus, Spiegelberg, Maspero, Leemans, mummy ticket, phonetics, transcription</vocab>
